<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is $7 ⋆ 5?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 \ \text{*} \ 5 \\\\
&= (7 - 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= 4 \times 5 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 \ \text{*} \ 5 \\\\
&= (7 - 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= 4 \times 5 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What values of $𝑎$ makes $𝑎 ⋆ 7 = 42?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \ \text{*} \ 7                                                   &= 42 \\\\
(a - 3) \times 7                                                   &= 42 \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {42} {7} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {6 \times 7} {7} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel{7}} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= 6 \\\\
a                                                                  &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a                                                                  &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
a \ \text{*} \ 7                                                   &= 42 \\\\
(a - 3) \times 7                                                   &= 42 \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {42} {7} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {6 \times 7} {7} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel{7}} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
a - 3                                                              &= 6 \\\\
a                                                                  &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a                                                                  &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $𝑏$ if $𝑏 ⋆ 𝑏 = 28$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{*} \ b                                                   &= 28 \\\\
(b - 3) \times b                                                   &= 28 
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $28$. 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $3$.

Factors of $28 = 1, 2, 4, 7, 14 \ \text{and} \ \ 28$

Factor pairs of $28$ that have difference of $3$ = ($7,4$)

| Factors   |  Product  |    Difference     |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $7, 4$    |   $28$    |       $3$         |

A quick check shows that $7$ and $4$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 7,4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7, 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{*} \ b                                                   &= 28 \\\\
(b - 3) \times b                                                   &= 28 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Product  |    Difference     |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $7, 4$    |   $28$    |       $3$         |
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 7,4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

I think of a number, multiply it by $5$, then subtract $3$. The result is $57$. What was the number I first thought of? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number I thought of $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 5 - 3                                      &= 57 \\\\
5a                                                  &= 57 + 3 \\\\
5a                                                  &= 60 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 12} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number I thought of $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 5 - 3                                      &= 57 \\\\
5a                                                  &= 57 + 3 \\\\
5a                                                  &= 60 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 12} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 37$

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$182 \div \ldots = 14$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
182 \div a                                          &= 14 \\\\
\dfrac{182}{a}                                      &= 14 \\\\
\dfrac{182}{14}                                     &= a \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {182} {14} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {13 \times 14} {14} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {13 \times \cancel{14}} {\cancel{14}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>

a                                                   &= \dfrac {13 \times \cancel{14}} {\cancel{14}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The missing number $= 13$
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>

As per the question,

$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{All sides of a pentagon have the same length}$.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3x + 5                                              &= 17 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 17 - 5 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 12 \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 4} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
x                                                   &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$4$

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 4) \times 6     &= 54  \\\\
a + 4                &= \dfrac{54}{6}  \\\\

a + 4                &= \dfrac{\cancel{6} \times 9}{\cancel{6}} \\\\
a + 4                &= 9 \\\\
a                    &= 9 - 4 \\\\
a                    &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Find $𝑏$ if $𝑏∎𝑏 = 32$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$b \times (b + 4) = 32$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $32$. 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $4$.

Factors of $32 = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32$

Factor pairs of $32$ that have a difference of $4 = (4, 8)$

| Factors   |  Prdouct  |    Difference     |
|:---------:|:---------:|:-----------------:|
| $4, 8$    |   $32$    |       $4$         |

A quick check shows that $4$ and $8$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $4$ and $8$.

$4$  and $8$

<div class='question question'>

Gary and Martin have some money. Martin has $\pounds 13$ more than Gary, together they have $\pounds 51$. How much money does Martin have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the money Gary has $= a$

Money Martin has $= 13 + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 13 + a                                              &= 51 \\\\                                  
2a                                                      &= 51 - 13 \\\\
2a                                                      &= 38 \\\\
2a                                                      &= 38 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 19} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 19} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= \pounds 19
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money Gary has}                                   &= \pounds 19 \\\\
\text{Money Martin has}                                 &= 19 + 13 \\\\
                                                        &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the money Gary has $= a$
<emptyline>
Money Martin has $= 13 + a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 13 + a                                              &= 51 \\\\                                  
2a                                                      &= 51 - 13 \\\\
2a                                                      &= 38 \\\\
2a                                                      &= 38 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 19} {2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 19} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= \pounds 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money Gary has}                                   &= \pounds 19 \\\\
\text{Money Martin has}                                 &= 19 + 13 \\\\
                                                        &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A new mathematical operation has been invented. For any two numbers $x ⊡ y$ means 'multiply $x$ by three, then add $y$', so $4 ⊡ 2$ means $4 \times 3 + 2 = 12 + 2 = 14$.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What is $6 ⊡ 4?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \times 3 + 4 \\\\
&= 18 + 4 \\\\
&= 22
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \times 3 + 4 \\\\
&= 18 + 4 \\\\
&= 22
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What values of 𝑎 makes $𝑎 ⊡ 5 = 29?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 + 5 &= 29 \\\\ 
3a             &= 29 - 5 \\\\ 
3a             &= 24 \\\\ 
a              &= \dfrac{24}{3} \\\\ 
a              &= \dfrac{8 \times 3}{3} \\\\ 
a              &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\ 
a              &= 8 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>

a              &= \dfrac{8 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\ 
a              &= 8 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
b \times 3 + b       &= 52 \\\\

b                    &= \dfrac{13 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
b                    &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

b                    &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x + 7                                              &= 46 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 46 - 7 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 39 \\\\

x                                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 13} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
x                                                   &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$13$

<div class='papername'>
<p>royal-russell-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:19</p>

<div class='question question'>

Alex and Emma buy the same type of bike.

Alex pays $\pounds169.99$ cash from his birthday money.

Emma pays a deposit of $\pounds70.00$ and then pays instalments of $\pounds10$ per month over the course of one year (12 months).

Who pays the most and by how much ? Show all of your working clearly.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The amount paid by alex}                   &= \pounds 169.99 \\\\
\text{The amount paid by Emma}                   &= 70 + 12 \times 10 \\\\ 
                                                 &= 70 + 120 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 190 \\\\
\text{The difference between the two prices}     &= 190 - 169.99 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 20.01
\end{aligned}
$

Emma paid an additional $\pounds20.01$ than Alex.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds20.01$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The amount paid by alex}                   &= \pounds 169.99 \\\\
\text{The amount paid by Emma}                   &= 70 + 12 \times 10 \\\\ 
                                                 &= 70 + 120 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 190 \\\\
\text{The difference between the two prices}     &= 190 - 169.99 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 20.01
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Emma paid an additional $\pounds20.01$ than Alex.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds20.01$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The entry prices at a theme park are: Adults $\pounds20.00$ & Children $\pounds15.00$.

A group visited the theme park and were charged $\pounds105.00$. How many adults & children were in the party? There was at least one adult.

Show all of your working clearly. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of adults $= a$

Let the number of children $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
20a + 15c     &= 105
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.

| $a$ |  $c$  | $20a + 15c$     |
|:---:|:-----:|----------------:|
|$2$  | $4$   | $100$           | 
|$4$  | $3$   | $85$            |
|$3$  | $3$   | $105$           |

A quick check shows that $a = 3$ and $c = 3$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of adults}     &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of children}   &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$ adults and $3$ children

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of adults $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number of children $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
20a + 15c     &= 105
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.
<emptyline>
| $a$ |  $c$  | $20a + 15c$     |
|:---:|:-----:|----------------:|
|$2$  | $4$   | $100$           | 
|$4$  | $3$   | $85$            |
|$3$  | $3$   | $105$           |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $a = 3$ and $c = 3$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of adults}     &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of children}   &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$ adults and $3$ children
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>360C3533-2ABA-4FD9-8CE1-6A9BFB2C6D9F</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>royal-russell-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:22</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Walker is planning a party for her grand-daughter and there will be $70$ children in total.

Each child will have a party bag to take home with a lollipop 
and a toy.

Empty party bags are sold in packs of $12$ and cost $\pounds2$ per pack.

Lollipops are sold in packs of $8$ and cost $\pounds4$ per pack.

Toys are sold in packs of $9$ and cost $\pounds10$ per pack.

As a special new year offer, all prices are reduced by one quarter.

How many packs of each item will she need to buy and what will be her total cost? Show all your working clearly. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{New cost of solid bag}                     &= 2 - 2 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1 \times 2}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1 \times \cancel{2}}{2 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  0.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 1.5 \\\\
\text{New cost of lollipops}                     &= 4 - 4 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - 4 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - \dfrac{4}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - \dfrac{\cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - 1 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 3 \\\\
\text{New cost of toys}                          &= 10 - 10 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{10}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5 \times 2}{2 \times 2} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5 \times \cancel{2}}{2 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5}{2} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - 2.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 7.5 \\\\
\text{Number of party bags required}             &= 70 \div 12 \\\\
												 &= \dfrac {70} {12}
                                                 &= 5 \text{ remainder } 10 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 6 \\\\
\text{Cost of party bags for }70\text{ children} &= 6 \times 1.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 9 \\\\
\text{Number of lollipop packs required}         &= 70 \div 8 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {70} {8} \\\\
                                                 &= 8 \text{ remainder } 6 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 9\\\\
\text{Cost of lollipops for }70\text{ children}  &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 27 \\\\
\text{Number of toy packs required}              &= 70 \div 9 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {70} {9} \\\\
                                                 &= 7 \text{ remainder } 7 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 8 \\\\
\text{Cost of toys for }70\text{ children}       &= 8 \times 7.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 60 \\\\
\text{Total cost}                                &= 60 + 27 + 9 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 95
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 95$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{New cost of solid bag}                     &= 2 - 2 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1 \times 2}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1 \times \cancel{2}}{2 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
                                                 &= 2 -  0.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 1.5 \\\\
\text{New cost of lollipops}                     &= 4 - 4 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - 4 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - \dfrac{4}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - \dfrac{\cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
                                                 &= 4 - 1 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 3 \\\\
\text{New cost of toys}                          &= 10 - 10 \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{10}{4} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5 \times 2}{2 \times 2} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5 \times \cancel{2}}{2 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - \dfrac{5}{2} \\\\
                                                 &= 10 - 2.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 7.5 \\\\
\text{Number of party bags required}             &= 70 \div 12 \\\\
												 &= \dfrac {70} {12}
                                                 &= 5 \text{ remainder } 10 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 6 \\\\
\text{Cost of party bags for }70\text{ children} &= 6 \times 1.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 9 \\\\
\text{Number of lollipop packs required}         &= 70 \div 8 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {70} {8} \\\\
                                                 &= 8 \text{ remainder } 6 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 9\\\\
\text{Cost of lollipops for }70\text{ children}  &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 27 \\\\
\text{Number of toy packs required}              &= 70 \div 9 \\\\
                                                 &= \dfrac {70} {9} \\\\
                                                 &= 7 \text{ remainder } 7 \\\\
                                                 &\approx 8 \\\\
\text{Cost of toys for }70\text{ children}       &= 8 \times 7.5 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 60 \\\\
\text{Total cost}                                &= 60 + 27 + 9 \\\\
                                                 &= \pounds 95
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 95$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$3x - 5 = 31$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x - 5 &= 31 \\\\
3x     &= 31 + 5 \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {12 \times 3 } {3} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3} } {\cancel{3} } \\\\
x      &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>

 x       &= -2
\end{aligned}

<div class='question subquestion'>

$\dfrac{x}{4} - 7 = 12$


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
 \dfrac {x} {4} - 7 &= 12 \\\\ 
 \dfrac {x} {4}     &= 12 + 7 \\\\ 
 \dfrac {x} {4}     &= 19 \\\\ 
 x                  &= 19 \times 4 \\\\ 
 x                  &= 76 
\end{aligned}   
$   

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$76$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
 \dfrac {x} {4} - 7 &= 12 \\\\ 
 \dfrac {x} {4}     &= 12 + 7 \\\\ 
 \dfrac {x} {4}     &= 19 \\\\ 
 x                  &= 19 \times 4 \\\\ 
 x                  &= 76 
\end{aligned}   
$

$\dfrac {x - 7} {4} = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {x - 7} {4} &= 12 \\\\
x - 7            &= 12 \times 4 \\\\
x - 7            &= 48 \\\\
x                &= 48 + 7 \\\\
x                &= 55
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55$

</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2010--sample-paper-1:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a box of pens, one half are black, one sixth are red and the rest are blue.  What fraction of the pens is blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total pens $= a$

Black pens $= \dfrac{a}{2}$

Red pens $= \dfrac{a}{6}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Blue pens}                 &= a - \dfrac {a} {2} - \dfrac {a} {6}    \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {6a - 3a - a} {6}             \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2a} {6}             \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel{2}a} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {a} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

Fraction of blue pens $= \dfrac{1}{3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total pens $= a$
<emptyline>
Black pens $= \dfrac{a}{2}$
<emptyline>
Red pens $= \dfrac{a}{6}$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Blue pens}                 &= a - \dfrac {a} {2} - \dfrac {a} {6}    \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {6a - 3a - a} {6}             \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2a} {6}             \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel{2}a} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {a} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Fraction of blue pens $= \dfrac{1}{3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(a \times 9) \div 6 = 6$ 

Find $a$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(a\times 9) \div 6     &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac{9a} {6}         &= 6 \\\\
9a                     &= 6 \times 6 \\\\
9a                     &= 36 \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 4} { \cancel {9} } \\\\
a                      &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
(a\times 9) \div 6     &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac{9a} {6}         &= 6 \\\\
9a                     &= 6 \times 6 \\\\
9a                     &= 36 \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 4} { \cancel {9} } \\\\
a                      &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(b + 6) \times 8 = 64$

Find $b$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(b + 6) \times 8   &= 64 \\\\
b + 6              &= \dfrac {64} {8} \\\\
b + 6              &= \dfrac {8 \times 8} {8} \\\\
b + 6              &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {8} } {\cancel {8} } \\\\
b + 6              &= 8 \\\\
b                  &= 8 - 6 \\\\
b                  &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

b + 6              &= \dfrac {64} {8} \\\\
b + 6              &= \dfrac {8 \times 8} {8} \\\\
b + 6              &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {8} } {\cancel {8} } \\\\
b + 6              &= 8 \\\\
b                  &= 8 - 6 \\\\
b                  &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>

$(19 \times c) + 25 = 101$

Find $c$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(19 \times c) + 25  &= 101 \\\\
19c                 &= 101 - 25 \\\\
c                   &= \dfrac {76} {19} \\\\
c                   &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {19} \\\\
c                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {19} \times 4} {\cancel {19} } \\\\
c                   &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

c                   &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find three consecutive numbers with a sum of $36$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Second consecutive number $= a + 1$

Third consecutive number $= a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + (a+1) + (a+2)  &= 36 \\\\
3a + 3             &= 36 \\\\
3a                 &= 36 - 3 \\\\
3a                 &= 33 \\\\
a                  &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
a                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
a                  &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 11} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
a                  &= 11
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {First number} 				 &= 11 \\\\
\text {Second consecutive number is} &= 11 + 1 \\\\
									 &= 12 \\\\
\text {Third consecutive number is}  &= 12 + 1 \\\\
									 &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

The three consecutive numbers are $11, 12, 13$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11, 12, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

a                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 11} {3} \\\\

a                  &= 11
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

									 &= 12 \\\\

									 &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The three consecutive numbers are $11, 12, 13$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find three consecutive numbers which give $336$ when multiplied together.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the consecutive numbers $= a, b, c$

As per the question,

$a \times b \times c = 336$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of $a, b,$ and $c$ which give a product of 336.

Factors of $336 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 12, 14, 16, 21, 24, 28, 42, 48, 56, 84, 112, 168, 336$

| $a, b, c$  | $a \times b \times c$  | Product = 64  |
|:----------:|:---------------------: |:------------: |
| $2, 3, 4$  |   $24$                 |    No         |
| $7, 8, 9$  |   $506$                |    NO         |
| $6, 7, 8$  |   $336$                |    Yes        |

A quick check shows that $6$, $7$ and $8$ meets all the criteria above.

Three consecutive numbers which give 36 when multiplied together $= 6, 7, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6, 7, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the consecutive numbers $= a, b, c$
<emptyline>

|:----------:|:---------------------: |:------------: |
| $2, 3, 4$  |   $24$                 |    No         |
| $7, 8, 9$  |   $506$                |    NO         |
| $6, 7, 8$  |   $336$                |    Yes        |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $6$, $7$ and $8$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
Three consecutive numbers which give 36 when multiplied together $= 6, 7, 8$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(x \times 18) \div 9 = 16$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(x \times 18) \div 9                                &= 16 \\\\
(x \times 18) \times \dfrac{1}{9}                   &= 16 \\\\
\dfrac {18x}{9}                                     &= 16 \\\\
18x                                                 &= 16 \times 9 \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {16 \times 9} {18} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 8 \times 9} {2 \times 9} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {9}} \\\\
x                                                   &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

18x                                                 &= 16 \times 9 \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {16 \times 9} {18} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 8 \times 9} {2 \times 9} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {9}} \\\\
x                                                   &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(x + 12) \times 4 + 2 = 62$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(x + 12) \times 4 + 2    &= 62 \\\\
(x + 12) \times 4        &= 62 - 2\\\\
(x + 12) \times 4        &= 60 \\\\
x + 12                   &= \dfrac {60}{4} \\\\
x + 12                   &= \dfrac {60}{4} \\\\
x + 12                   &= \dfrac {15 \times 4} {4} \\\\
x + 12                   &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {4} } {\cancel {4} } \\\\
x + 12                   &= 15 \\\\
x                        &= 15 - 12 \\\\
x                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

x + 12                   &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {4} } {\cancel {4} } \\\\
x + 12                   &= 15 \\\\
x                        &= 15 - 12 \\\\
x                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$(5x - 3) - (4x - 5) = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(5x - 3) - (4x - 5) &= 12 \\\\
(5x - 3) -4x + 5    &= 12 \\\\
x + 2               &= 12 \\\\
x                   &= 12 - 2 \\\\
x                   &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$5(x - 3) + 4 (x- 2) = 13$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
5(x - 3) + 4(x - 2)                &= 13 \\\\
5x - 5 \times 3 + 4x - 4 \times 2  &= 13 \\\\
5x - 15 + 4x - 8                   &= 13 \\\\
9x -23                             &= 13 \\\\
9x                                 &= 13 + 23 \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9} \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac { \cancel{9} \times 4} {\cancel {9} } \\\\
x                                  &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

5x - 15 + 4x - 8                   &= 13 \\\\
9x -23                             &= 13 \\\\
9x                                 &= 13 + 23 \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9} \\\\
x                                  &= \dfrac { \cancel{9} \times 4} {\cancel {9} } \\\\
x                                  &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$4x - 3 = 21$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
4x - 3 &= 21 \\\\
4x     &= 21 + 3 \\\\
4x     &= 24 \\\\\
x      &= \dfrac{24}{4}  \\\\
x      &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel {4} }{\cancel {4} } \\\\
x      &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

4x     &= 21 + 3 \\\\
4x     &= 24 \\\\\
x      &= \dfrac{24}{4}  \\\\
x      &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel {4} }{\cancel {4} } \\\\
x      &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$3 ( x + 10) = 60$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3(x + 10)  &= 60 \\\\
x + 10     &= \dfrac{60}{3} \\\\
x + 10     &= \dfrac{3 \times 20}{3} \\\\
x + 10     &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 20}{ \cancel{3} } \\\\
x + 10     &=  20 \\\\
x          &= 20 - 10 \\\\
x          &= 10
\end{aligned}
$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

x          &= 20 - 10 \\\\
x          &= 10
\end{aligned}
$ 
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
5x + 1    &= 2x + 7 \\\\
5x - 2x   &= 7 - 1 \\\\

x         &= \dfrac {6} {3} \\\\
x         &= \dfrac{2 \times \cancel {3} }{\cancel {3} } \\\\
x         &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

\begin{aligned}

x                       &= \dfrac  {8 \times 3} {3} \\\\
x                       &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel{3} } {\cancel {3} } \\\\
x                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Albert, Brian and Charlie are dogs. Albert and Brian weigh $22 \ \text{kg}$ together. Brian and Charlie weigh $20 \ \text{kg}$ together. All three dogs weigh $30 \ \text{kg}$. How much does each dog weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of Albert, Brian, and Charlie is A, B, and C respectively.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
A + B     &= 22 \ \text{kg} \\\\
B + C     &= 20 \ \text{kg} \\\\
A + B + C &= 30 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Lets, first find value of A

$
\begin{aligned}
A + (B + C)  &= 30 \\\\
A + 20       &= 30 \\\\
A            &= 30 - 20 \\\\
A            &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Lets use the value of A to find B,

$
\begin{aligned}
A + B  &= 22 \\\\
B      &= 22 - A \\\\
B      &= 22 - 10 \\\\
B      &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Lets use the value of B to find C,

$
\begin{aligned}
B + C &= 20 \\\\
C     &= 20 - B \\\\
C     &= 20 - 11 \\\\
C     &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Albert $=10$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Brian $= 11$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Charlie $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of Albert, Brian, and Charlie is A, B, and C respectively.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

\end{aligned}
$

<emptyline>

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

C     &= 20 - B \\\\
C     &= 20 - 11 \\\\
C     &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>4FD3FA7F-457F-4731-B4AE-15DCF80081B3</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Tim, David and Alison are given some money by their grandfather. Tim gets twice as much as David and three times as much as Alison. If they get $\pounds 49.50$ in total, how much do they each get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the money Tim received $= a$

Money David recieved $= \dfrac{a}{2}$

Money Alison recieved $= \dfrac{a}{3}$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + \dfrac {a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {3}                                                                     &= 49.50 \\\\
\dfrac {6 \times a} {1 \times 6} + \dfrac {a \times 3} {2 \times 3} + \dfrac {a \times 2} {3 \times 2}  &= 49.50 \\\\
\dfrac {6a} {6} + \dfrac {3a} {6} + \dfrac {2a} {6}                                                     &= 49.50 \\\\
\dfrac {6a + 3a + 2a} {6}                                                                               &= 49.50 \\\\
11a                                                                                                     &= 49.50 \times 6 \\\\
a                                                                                                       &= \dfrac {297} {11} \\\\
a                                                                                                       &= \dfrac {27 \times 11} {11} \\\\
a                                                                                                       &= \dfrac {27 \times \cancel {11}} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a                                                                                                       &= \pounds 27
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money Tim received}       &= \pounds 27 \\\\
\text{Money David recieved}     &= \dfrac {27}{2} \\\\ 
                                &= \pounds 13.5 \\\\
\text{Money Alison recieved}    &= \dfrac {27}{3} \\\\ 
                                &= \pounds 9
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

Tom $= \pounds27$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

David $= \pounds13.5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Alison $= \pounds9$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<emptyline>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-5:1:8</p>

talgebra

<div class='question question'>

Two numbers have a sum of $87$. The larger of the numbers is twice the smaller. What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the smaller number $= a$

Larger number $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a                      &= 87 \\\\
3a                          &= 87 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {87} {3} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {29 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {29 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                           &= 29 \\\\
\text{Smaller Number}       &= 29 \\\\
\text{Larger number}        &= 2 \times 29 \\\\
                            &= 58
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29, 58$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the smaller number $= a$
<emptyline>
Larger number $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a                      &= 87 \\\\
3a                          &= 87 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {87} {3} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {29 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {29 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                           &= 29 \\\\
\text{Smaller Number}       &= 29 \\\\
\text{Larger number}        &= 2 \times 29 \\\\
                            &= 58
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$29, 58$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$5x - 8 = 27$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
5x - 8 &= 27 \\\\
5x     &= 27 + 8 \\\\
5x     &= 35 \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 7} {\cancel {5} } \\\\
x      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
5x - 8 &= 27 \\\\
5x     &= 27 + 8 \\\\
5x     &= 35 \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
x      &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 7} {\cancel {5} } \\\\
x      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$5(2x + 4) = 50$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
5(2x + 4)    &= 50   \\\\
2x + 4       &= \dfrac {50} {5} \\\\
2x + 4       &= \dfrac {5 \times 10} {5} \\\\
2x + 4       &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 10} {\cancel {5} } \\\\
2x + 4       &= 10 \\\\
2x           &= 10 - 4 \\\\
x            &= 6 \\\\
x            &= \dfrac{2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
x            &= \dfrac{\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2} } \\\\
x            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

2x + 4       &= \dfrac {5 \times 10} {5} \\\\
2x + 4       &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 10} {\cancel {5} } \\\\
2x + 4       &= 10 \\\\
2x           &= 10 - 4 \\\\
x            &= 6 \\\\
x            &= \dfrac{2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
x            &= \dfrac{\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2} } \\\\
x            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$12x = 5x + 28$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
12x      &= 5x + 28 \\\\
12x - 5x &= 28 \\\\
7x       &= 28 \\\\
x        &= \dfrac {28} {7} \\\\
x        &= \dfrac {7 \times 4} {7} \\\\ 
x        &= \dfrac {\cancel {7} \times 4} {\cancel {7} } \\\\ 
x        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

$4$

x        &= \dfrac {7 \times 4} {7} \\\\ 
x        &= \dfrac {\cancel {7} \times 4} {\cancel {7} } \\\\ 
x        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {5x} {4} + 3 &= 13 \\\\
\dfrac {5x} {4}     &= 13 - 3 \\\\
\dfrac {5x} {4}     &= 10 \\\\
5x                  &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
5x                  &= 40 \\\\
x                   &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
x                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {5} \\\\
x                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 8} {\cancel {5} } \\\\
x                   &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

x                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {5} \\\\

x                   &= 8

<div class='question question'>

Gavin spends $\pounds 4.90$ on two items in a shop. One item cost $40 \ \text{p}$ more than the other.  How much did each item cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Let the cost of First item}  &= a \\\\
\text{The cost of second item}     &= a + 40 \ \text{p} \\\\ 
                                   &= a + \pounds \dfrac{40}{100} \\\\
                                   &= a + 0.4
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 0.40                                      &= 4.90  \\\\
2a + 0.40                                         &= 4.90 \\\\
2a                                                &= 4.90 - 0.40 \\\\
2a                                                &= 4.50 \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {4.50} {2} \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {2.25 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {2.25 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                 &= \pounds 2.25
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of the first item}      &= \pounds 2.25 \\\\
\text{Cost of second item }        &= a + 0.40 \\\\
                                   &= 2.25 + 0.40 \\\\
                                   &= 2.65 \\\\
                                   &= \pounds 2.65
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.65$ and $\pounds 2.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Let the cost of First item}  &= a \\\\
\text{The cost of second item}     &= a + 40 \ \text{p} \\\\ 
                                   &= a + \pounds \dfrac{40}{100} \\\\
                                   &= a + 0.4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2a + 0.40                                         &= 4.90 \\\\
2a                                                &= 4.90 - 0.40 \\\\
2a                                                &= 4.50 \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {4.50} {2} \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {2.25 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                                 &= \dfrac {2.25 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                 &= \pounds 2.25
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of the first item}      &= \pounds 2.25 \\\\
\text{Cost of second item }        &= a + 0.40 \\\\
                                   &= 2.25 + 0.40 \\\\
                                   &= 2.65 \\\\
                                   &= \pounds 2.65
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.65$ and $\pounds 2.25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Calculate the total length marked with $x$.

![sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2015--sample-paper-6/section-1-question-7-00.png](/assets/sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2015--sample-paper-6/section-1-question-7-00.png "sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2015--sample-paper-6/section-1-question-7-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac{1}{3} \times 15  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac {5 \times 3} {3}  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}}  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + 5  \\\\
x                        &= 20 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

The total length marked with $x$ is $20 \ \text{cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac{1}{3} \times 15  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac {5 \times 3} {3}  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}}  \\\\
x                        &= 15 + 5  \\\\
x                        &= 20 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The total length marked with $x$ is $20 \ \text{cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$39$ members of Alwick Youth Club went on an outing to a leisure centre. They went in minibuses which could seat up to $15$ members.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many minibuses were needed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Minibuses required}   &= \dfrac {39} {15} \\\\
                            &= 2 \text{ remainder } 9 \\\\
                            &\approx 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$ minibuses

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Minibuses required}   &= \dfrac {39} {15} \\\\
                            &= 2 \text{ remainder } 9 \\\\
                            &\approx 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$ minibuses
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many spare seats were there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the answer above, there were $9$ members in the $3$rd bus.

A bus could seat up to $15$ members.

So, $15 - 9 = 6$

There were $6$ spares seats.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$ seats

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the answer above, there were $9$ members in the $3$rd bus.
<emptyline>
A bus could seat up to $15$ members.
<emptyline>
So, $15 - 9 = 6$
<emptyline>
There were $6$ spares seats.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$ seats
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The transport costs were $\pounds 90$ altogether. They also had to pay $\pounds 150$ for the group to use the leisure centre. Helen collected $\pounds 6.50$ from each passenger to pay for this.

How much was left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Expenses}        &= \ \text{Transport cost} + \text{Leisure centre costs} \\\\
                             &= 90 + 150 \\\\
                             &= \pounds 240
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left}        &= \ \text{Total money collected} - \text{Total Expenses} \\\\
                         &= (39 \times 6.50) - 240 \\\\
                         &= 253.5 - 240 \\\\
                         &= \pounds 13.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 13.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Expenses}        &= \ \text{Transport cost} + \text{Leisure centre costs} \\\\
                             &= 90 + 150 \\\\
                             &= \pounds 240
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left}        &= \ \text{Total money collected} - \text{Total Expenses} \\\\
                         &= (39 \times 6.50) - 240 \\\\
                         &= 253.5 - 240 \\\\
                         &= \pounds 13.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 13.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>3BADA08D-7FEF-4ADD-98DC-7E1F438D14C6</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7:1:4</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Michelle has the same number of $10$p and $50$p coins. The total value of the coins is $\pounds 9$. How many of each coin does she have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of $10$p coins $= a$

Number of $50$p coins $= a$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10\ \text{p} \times a + 50\ \text{p} \times a         &= \pounds9 \\\\
10\ \text{p} \times a + 50\ \text{p} \times a         &= 9 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
10a + 50a                         				  &= 900\\\\
60a                               				  &= 900 \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {900} {60} \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {15 \times 60 }{60} \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {60} } {\cancel {60}} \\\\
a                                 				  &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins $10$p coin $= 15$

Number of coins $50$p coin $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$ coins of each $10$p and $50$p

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of $10$p coins $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of $50$p coins $= a$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
As per question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10\ \text{p} \times a + 50\ \text{p} \times a         &= \pounds9 \\\\
10\ \text{p} \times a + 50\ \text{p} \times a         &= 9 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
10a + 50a                         				  &= 900\\\\
60a                               				  &= 900 \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {900} {60} \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {15 \times 60 }{60} \\\\
a                                 				  &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {60} } {\cancel {60}} \\\\
a                                 				  &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins $10$p coin $= 15$
<emptyline>
Number of coins $50$p coin $= 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$ coins of each $10$p and $50$p
</code></pre>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>D5FCF383-D5AD-496F-B028-D70D36DF39D1</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7:1:20</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The mean of four numbers $x, 4, 5$ and $y$  is $5$ and the range of the numbers is $7$.  Find $x$ and  $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{x + 4 + 5 + y}{4}        &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac{9 + x + y}{4}            &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac{9 + x + y}{4}            &= 5 \\\\
9 + x + y                       &= 5 \times 4 \\\\
9 + x + y                       &= 20 \\\\
x + y                           &= 20 -  9 \\\\
x + y                           &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Lets find out the value of $x$ and $y$ which satisfies the condition:

- The range is $7$
- $x + y = 11$
 
| x |       y      |         Range      |
|:-:|:------------:|:------------------:|
|$1$|  $11-1 = 10$ |       10 - 1 = 9   |
|$2$|  $11-2 = 9$  |       9 - 2 = 7    |
|$3$|  $11-3 = 8$  |       8 - 3 = 5    |
|$4$|  $11-4 = 7$  |       7 - 4 = 3    |
|$5$|  $11-5 = 6$  |       6 - 4 = 2    |
|$6$|  $11-6 = 5$  |       6 - 4 = 2    |

A quick check shows that $2$ and $9$ meets the criteria above.

$x = 2$

$y = 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$ and $9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{x + 4 + 5 + y}{4}        &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac{9 + x + y}{4}            &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac{9 + x + y}{4}            &= 5 \\\\
9 + x + y                       &= 5 \times 4 \\\\
9 + x + y                       &= 20 \\\\
x + y                           &= 20 -  9 \\\\
x + y                           &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets find out the value of $x$ and $y$ which satisfies the condition:
<emptyline>
- The range is $7$
- $x + y = 11$
<emptyline>
| x |       y      |         Range      |
|:-:|:------------:|:------------------:|
|$1$|  $11-1 = 10$ |       10 - 1 = 9   |
|$2$|  $11-2 = 9$  |       9 - 2 = 7    |
|$3$|  $11-3 = 8$  |       8 - 3 = 5    |
|$4$|  $11-4 = 7$  |       7 - 4 = 3    |
|$5$|  $11-5 = 6$  |       6 - 4 = 2    |
|$6$|  $11-6 = 5$  |       6 - 4 = 2    |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $2$ and $9$ meets the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$x = 2$
<emptyline>
$y = 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2$ and $9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7:1:21</p>

<div class='topics'>

talgebra
</li>
<li>
tnumber
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Dini thought of a number. She doubled this number and added $10$ to give the result $52$. What number did Dini think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number Dini thought of $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 10   &= 52 \\\\
2a + 10           &= 52 \\\\
2a                &= 52 - 10 \\\\
2a                &= 42 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {42} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {21 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

The number Dini thought of $= 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number Dini thought of $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 10   &= 52 \\\\
2a + 10           &= 52 \\\\
2a                &= 52 - 10 \\\\
2a                &= 42 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {42} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {21 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number Dini thought of $= 21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

To make eight $200 \ \text{ml}$ glasses of squash, Yasmin needs $320 \ \text{ml}$ of cordial; the rest is water.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much cordial does Yasmin need to make three glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let cordial needed to make one glasses of squash $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
8 \times \text {a}                                                &= 320 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {320} {8} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {8 \times 40} {8} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 40} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 40 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$

Yasmin need $40 \ \text {ml}$ of cordial to make one glass of squash. So,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cordial needed to make three glasses of squash}   &= 3 \times 40 \\\\
                                                         &= 120 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$ 

Yasmin need $120 \ \text {ml}$ of cordial to make three glasses of squash.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \ \text {ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let cordial needed to make one glasses of squash $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
8 \times \text {a}                                                &= 320 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {320} {8} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {8 \times 40} {8} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 40} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 40 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Yasmin need $40 \ \text {ml}$ of cordial to make one glass of squash. So,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cordial needed to make three glasses of squash}   &= 3 \times 40 \\\\
                                                         &= 120 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$ 
<emptyline>
Yasmin need $120 \ \text {ml}$ of cordial to make three glasses of squash.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120 \ \text {ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much water does Yasmin need to make ten glasses of squash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Water needed to make one glass of squash}  &= \text {One glass of squash} - \text {Cordial needed to make one glass of squash} \\\\
                                                  &= 200 - 40 \\\\
                                                  &= 160 \ \text {ml} \\\\
\text {Water needed to make ten glasses of squash}  &= 10 \times 160 \\\\
                                                    &= 1600 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$

Yasmin need $1600 \ \text {ml}$ of water to make ten glasses of squash.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1600 \ \text {ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Water needed to make one glass of squash}  &= \text {One glass of squash} - \text {Cordial needed to make one glass of squash} \\\\
                                                  &= 200 - 40 \\\\
                                                  &= 160 \ \text {ml} \\\\
\text {Water needed to make ten glasses of squash}  &= 10 \times 160 \\\\
                                                    &= 1600 \ \text {ml}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Yasmin need $1600 \ \text {ml}$ of water to make ten glasses of squash.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1600 \ \text {ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-8:1:14</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='question question'>

Rex has $80$ books altogether. They are arranged on three shelves. If Rex moved seven books from the top shelf to the middle shelf and took eight books away from the bottom shelf, there would be an equal number of books on each shelf. 

How many books are on the middle shelf originally?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of books in middle shelf $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 7  &= \dfrac{80-8}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{72}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{24 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{24 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
a + 7  &= 24 \\\\
a      &= 24 - 7 \\\\
a      &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

There are originally 17 books in the middle shelf.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of books in middle shelf $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 7  &= \dfrac{80-8}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{72}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{24 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a + 7  &= \dfrac{24 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
a + 7  &= 24 \\\\
a      &= 24 - 7 \\\\
a      &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
There are originally 17 books in the middle shelf.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-8:1:18</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
